2026-07-30
probe sidecar: ask the geometry and stability questions off the GPU box
ckpt.pt carries the memory store (MEM_CAP x KW floats) and both optimizers' moments, so at D=768 /
MEM_CAP=200000 it runs to gigabytes -- fine on the machine that wrote it, impractical to move off a rented
GPU. But probe_ckpt_geometry and probe_stability read FOUR things: the signature encoder, the domain
centroids, SIG_D and WIN. Every save now also writes probe.pt with just those, and both probes prefer it
when handed a run directory. Measured on a D=64 run: 187 KB against 18 MB, a 97x cut, and it scales the
same way (it is O(vocab x D), not O(MEM_CAP)).

This makes the split explicit: the GH200 trains and drops probe.pt; every question about what was learned
gets asked anywhere, later, without the GPU.

Verified end-to-end on two CPU seeds. probe_stability RAN TO COMPLETION for the first time in this project
-- it had never once produced a number:

  AGREEMENT A vs B (NMI)                        0.757
  shuffled-B floor (B's cluster sizes, no correspondence)  0.002  [0.000-0.005 over 20 draws]
  agreement with the SEEDED corpora:  A 0.655 | B 0.760

The two runs agree with EACH OTHER (0.757) more than run A agrees with the categories we spliced in
(0.655). That is the discovery signature, and it is the first direct evidence bearing on how arbitrary our
four domains are: the structure is reproducible across seeds and is not identical to our labels.

CAVEAT, loudly: this is a 60 KB / D=64 / 3-domain CPU run, not the 4 MB configuration. It establishes that
the probe works and what the number looks like. It does not establish the finding at scale.

2026-07-30
the ablation arms were never run, so two of them were broken
ab_no_world exited 1 and produced no data: WORLD_GROW defaults ON and its step hook called world_fwd.n()
outside the `if WORLD_MODEL:` block, so WORLD_MODEL=0 died at the first MANAGE_EVERY. The one ablation that
would have said what the world model is worth was the one that could not run.

Rather than fix it alone, added `bash rerun.sh smoke`: every arm at 40 KB on CPU, exit codes only, numbers
deliberately not printed so nobody reads them as results. It immediately found a second broken arm --
SIG_SPACE=tokens crashed in the eval battery. The training loop converted the loop index into ENC_SEQ
correctly (`i if SIG_SPACE == "tokens" else bpos`); every EVAL site did `tok_bs[s]` unconditionally, which
under SIG_SPACE=tokens scales a token index by ~2.5 and reads a window from the wrong place. That is
silently wrong first and loudly wrong only at the tail, when the offset runs off the end of ENC_SEQ. Both
conversions now go through one helper (encpos/encwin).

Also:
- rerun.sh eng arm gets SEG_MIN/SEG_MAX. seg_from() draws each segment from a RANDOM OFFSET in the corpus,
  so on ONE corpus the 700/1800 default makes the stream jump elsewhere in English every ~1250 B = 3.3
  analysis windows. The 71 domains that arm assembled are partly a count of splices we introduced.
- rerun.sh prints an ABLATION TABLE across arms. Each report's "model ALONE -> +FABRIC" is an eval-time
  KNOCKOUT of a component the model trained with, and it carries its own caveat that it overstates; the
  honest comparison is one arm against another. Using the knockout number (+0.709) to justify defaulting
  FABRIC ON was exactly that mistake -- the retrained ablation says 3.089 vs 3.090, no bits/byte at all.
  The default holds on coherence (0.75 vs 0.50) and drift (+0.132 vs +0.154) instead.

prep the reruns: catch the store-size override, add a launcher, widen the knob trap
Turning MEM_PER_EXPERT on silently shrank the memory 24x and nothing said so. memory.py:35 reads
`if self.n_own > 1: cap = self.n_own * self.quota`, so at the defaults FAB_NMAX=64 and MEM_QUOTA=128
the store is 8192 slots and MEM_CAP=200000 is discarded. Every memory result scales with that number.
It now warns, states the reduction factor, and names the MEM_QUOTA that would preserve the requested
cap -- while leaving the small-quota design available as a deliberate choice rather than an accident.

rerun.sh runs the reruns the subsystem audit made necessary, with the settings inline so shell quoting
cannot mangle them (it mangled two of my earlier grids). Three modes: the 4-corpus stream, the single
English corpus, and an ABLATION arm that removes one subsystem at a time from the full stack --
fabric, world model, per-expert memory, tokenizer, domains. That last measurement could not exist
while they were all off, and it is the honest way to justify each default rather than asserting it.

It also fixes MEM_QUOTA=3125 so the store stays at 200000, and prints the read-order: ANCHORS and
COHERENCE first because they speak to proper language; RETENTION, LEARNING CURVE, FABRIC and the
domain-prior arms next because they explain why those moved. Domain counts, purity, silhouette and
V-measure are labelled DIAGNOSTICS in the header, with a note that steering by them is what produced
most of this file's history.

preflight.sh's knob trap now covers everything the audit touched (PHASED, ENC_VREG, DOM_PRIOR,
MEM_QUOTA, WORLD_GROW, SIG_SPACE and the rest) so an unread knob cannot hide the way D_MODEL_B did.

Validated the launcher end to end on CPU: exit 0, no tracebacks, config warnings surfacing, and all
the headline lines extracted.

51889b7a4f6034fcf1e7161f89da7aaca1025222
2026-07-29
every subsystem on by default -- five more were off besides the fabric
The audit that found FABRIC=0 found five more flags defaulting off, so the "full system" this project
has been measuring was the base LM plus memory plus domains and nothing else:

  TOKENIZER / TOK_ONLINE   the expanding byte-BPE           OFF
  WORLD_MODEL              forward-dynamics population      OFF
  WORLD_GROW               its plateau growth               OFF
  WORLD_FEEDBACK           folding it into model.encode      OFF
  MEM_PER_EXPERT           the per-expert memory partition   OFF   <- explicitly requested design
  FABRIC                   the routed expert population      OFF   (fixed last commit)

A flag that defaults off is a decision nobody makes and everybody inherits. Same failure as PHASED=0,
MANAGE_MERGE=0.12, SEG_MIN/WIN and the BATCH_W cadences. Off is now the deliberate ablation.

Verified with all of them on together (4 corpora, BATCH_W=16, 100 kB): exit 0, no tracebacks, no
skipped sections, and all eleven report sections present -- ANCHORS, RETENTION, LEARNING CURVE,
COHERENCE, CAN A DOMAIN PREDICT, IS THE PARTITION INFORMATIVE, NON-STATIONARY, WORLD MODEL, FABRIC,
AFFILIATION, and the per-expert memory line ("64 owners x 128 entries = 8192 slots, LRU by last USE").

STILL OFF, for reasons rather than by oversight:
  EXPERTS       mutually exclusive with FABRIC -- the forward pass is an elif chain and FABRIC wins, so
                enabling both makes the expert bank a silent no-op. The exclusivity is arguably a bug;
                until it composes, FABRIC carries the routing.
  DISK_STREAM   a data-source choice, not a subsystem, and it fails with no corpora on disk.
  DOM_ADAPTIVE, DOM_RELATIVE, SHIFT_REL   each measured WORSE than the constant it replaces.

One thing the full stack surfaces immediately: the world model reports "beats baseline -84.7% | latent
std 0.07", which by its own printed criterion (std ~0 = collapsed) means it has NOT learned dynamics.
It has never been run, so this is its first reading, not a regression.

the router fabric was OFF in every run of this project
FABRIC defaulted to 0. Nobody set it. So the routed expert population -- the core of the architecture
-- was absent from every run: "fabric nodes 0" in every phase table, no FABRIC section in any report,
and every conclusion this project has drawn about domains, coherence and bits/byte was measured on a

cadences, and I did not check it.

Measured, English, 120 kB, everything else identical:

  FABRIC=0   held-out 3.543   LOSES to order-1 (3.495) by 0.048
  FABRIC=1   held-out 3.441   BEATS order-1 by 0.054
  fabric contributes +0.709 bits/byte (3.905 -> 3.196), four times what the memory contributes

That is the largest single component effect measured in this project, and it flips the sign of the
comparison against a bigram table -- the one anchor that says whether any of this is doing work.

the time and mean routed depth is 0.10 of 4 steps, with node mass concentrated on almost nothing. So

are separate problems, and only the first is fixed here.

The affiliation map is populated and immediately useful: 18 experts serve more than one domain, 8 are
exclusive, and 10 SERVE NO DOMAIN AT ALL. That last number is dead capacity, and it is exactly the
signal domain-driven expert discovery would consume.

7b481a1cfc642e4ad73590b01370bef050ea2fba
2026-07-29
make domains available to PREDICTION, and measure whether they earn it
Domains were only ever an editing index: `did` reaches mem.src (provenance), dom_exp (reporting) and
the clustering report, and NOTHING in the prediction path reads it -- retrieval is a global kNN over
memory keys, routing runs on the continuous gist. So the partition could not help prediction, because
prediction never asked.

One route was already closed by measurement. Conditioning RETRIEVAL on the domain is dead: restricting
to the query's own domain beats a foreign domain by no more than a shuffled-provenance null, i.e. the
label carries nothing the memory keys do not already encode.

A PRIOR is a different claim -- not "which stored entry is similar" but "in this kind of text, which
tokens are likely at all". The anchors say a global order-0 model is worth something, so a sharper
per-domain one should be worth more IF domains are real. Each domain now accumulates a token
histogram (counts follow merges and are dropped on cull, like memory provenance), and DOM_PRIOR
blends it into the prediction.

Evaluated on HELD-OUT text -- a per-domain histogram would trivially win on the training windows it
counted -- with eval windows assigned the way the assembler actually assigns them (encode, nearest
centroid), never by which memory entry happens to be nearest. Four arms, because two nulls are needed:

  model alone | + GLOBAL prior | + OWN-domain prior | + RANDOM-domain prior

OWN must beat GLOBAL to show the PARTITION adds anything over plain frequency, and must beat RANDOM
to show the LABEL is doing it rather than the blend. Measured:

  eng only, 31 domains   3.503 | 3.539 | 3.523 | 3.524   own-global +0.016  own-random +0.000
  4 corpora, 6 domains   3.912 | 3.970 | 3.919 | 3.982   own-global +0.050  own-random +0.063
  4 corpora, weight 0.05 3.912 | 3.928 | 3.910 | 3.932   own-global +0.018  own-random +0.021

On four distinct corpora the label predicts: the right domain's histogram beats both a global one and
a wrong one. On a single English corpus it does not -- own and random are identical to three decimals,
so the apparent edge over the global histogram is only "sharper", not "correct". Without the
random-domain arm that would have read as a success.

Note also that every prior arm is WORSE than the model alone at these weights, so this is not yet a
gain to bank -- it is evidence that the label carries usable signal when the domains differ in kind,
and none when they do not.

give the informativeness null an error bar -- the verdict was flipping on noise
Two 4 MB English runs differing only in SEED came back at excess +0.010 and +0.013 against a hard
cutoff of 0.010, and printed opposite conclusions: "NOT distinguishable from a random partition" and
"the partition CARRIES INFORMATION". The threshold was inside its own noise band.

The null was a single permutation, so it had no spread to compare against. It now runs INFO_NULLS
(default 5) permutations and reports mean +/- sd, and the verdict requires the excess to clear 2
sigma. Measured on the same material: null spread +/-0.020, excess +0.000. Both GPU runs sit well
inside +/-2 sigma = +/-0.040, so both are correctly NOT distinguishable, and the disagreement was
never real.

The negative result stands and is worth stating plainly: with 64-68 well-formed, recurring,
boundary-detecting domains assembled from a SINGLE English corpus, the partition carries no
predictive information beyond a random partition of the same shape.

That is consistent with the structure of the code rather than a defect in the domains. `did` is
consumed by mem.src (provenance), dom_exp (reporting) and the clustering report. NOTHING in the
prediction path reads it: retrieval is a global kNN over memory keys, and fabric/expert routing runs
on the continuous gist. The domain label cannot help prediction because prediction never asks for it.
If domains are meant to earn their keep predictively, something in that path has to use them.

c1aadda71aaf37a876fd999544793234000a3859
2026-07-29
the signature encoder was collapsing on homogeneous text; the other corpora were hiding it
Going English-only exposed the real defect, and it inverts the hypothesis that the extra corpora were
throwing the system off. They were the only thing PREVENTING a collapse.

  DOMAINS=eng          encoder loss plateau 3.83 / 3.78     ln(ENC_BATCH=48) = 3.871
  DOMAINS=eng,py,num,c encoder loss plateau 2.10 / 2.18

ln(batch) is exactly the loss of an encoder that emits ONE CONSTANT VECTOR. InfoNCE draws its
negatives from the same stream, so with a single corpus there are no cross-kind negatives and the
trivial solution is reachable; with four there always are, and it is not. Textbook contrastive
collapse, and it took a single-corpus run to see it. Downstream: separation 0.16 -> 0.05, SHIFT_DIST
never fires, 0 boundaries, 1 domain, and a report page of purity 1.00 / homogeneity 1.00 /
V-measure 1.00 / silhouette +0.95 / "1/1 GENUINE", because a partition of one is perfect.

The remedy was already in the repository. _var_cov (world_model.py) is a VICReg-style variance +
decorrelation penalty, used for the dynamics population and never applied to the encoder that
actually collapses. One correction was needed: its variance hinge targets std >= 1, unreachable for
L2-NORMALISED outputs, since a uniform unit vector in SIG_D dims has per-dim std 1/sqrt(SIG_D). Scale
by sqrt(SIG_D) first and a well-spread signature space sits exactly at the hinge.

Measured, English only, 80 kB:
  ENC_VREG   live domains   separation(end)   encoder loss
    0 (was)       2             0.01          3.85  <- ln(48): trivial solution
    1.0           5             0.44          3.70
    5.0          17             0.97          1.69  <- actually solving the task
    5.0+CREG     24             0.96          1.85

Two follow-ups worth having in the record. The CONSTANT SHIFT_DIST works again once the scale is
healthy (13 domains, SHIFT_REL not needed) -- SHIFT_REL had merely been compensating for a collapsed
space, which is why it read as a wash on 4 corpora. And the cost on mixed material is small: V 0.56
-> 0.52, 4.322 -> 4.384 bits/byte.

Defaulted ON at 5.0. The production target is one large corpus, where collapse is a certainty rather
than a risk, so a small cost on a mixture we are moving away from is the right trade. Single run per
cell; the value is a measured plateau, not a tuned optimum.

d460e92c91ad5a43bcfa3d5e3bc88dba6e669cc8
2026-07-29
detect encoder collapse -- the adaptive warmup could not tell convergence from it
The two single-corpus runs at 4 MB produced 0 boundaries and 1 domain, and every domain metric on the
page still printed and still looked fine: purity 1.00, homogeneity 1.00, V-measure 1.00, silhouette
+0.95, "1/1 GENUINE". A partition of one is perfect by construction. The cause is in the encoder
curve, and the stop condition read it backwards:

  DOMAINS=eng   0:6.77:0.16   500:3.83:0.05
  (adaptive warmup: STOPPED EARLY at 501/2000 on separation plateau)

Signature separation fell 0.16 -> 0.05, a 69% COLLAPSE, and the stop -- `_sep <= _prev_sep*(1+eps)`
-- is equally true of a flat curve and a falling one. It called that convergence and stopped. After
which SHIFT_DIST never fired, the assembler never created a second domain, and the whole domain
apparatus sat inert behind a page of perfect scores.

This is the expected failure on HOMOGENEOUS material, not a coincidence: InfoNCE draws its negatives
from the same stream, so with one corpus there are no cross-kind negatives and nothing resists the
representation shrinking to a point. It is also exactly the condition under which the domain question
matters most, so it needed to be loud.

Now tracks peak separation and warns when the final value is below 70% of it or below 0.15 absolute,
naming the consequence (~0 boundaries, 1 domain, downstream metrics valid and meaningless) and the
levers (wider material, ENC_PROTO, SIG_SPACE). Verified: DOMAINS=eng reproduces 0.46 -> 0.07 and now
prints !! ENCODER COLLAPSE alongside the 1-domain result.

probe_stability.py gets the matching guard. With one domain per run it would have computed NMI over a
constant labelling, which is 0 by construction, and printed "NO MORE THAN CHANCE" -- a finding-shaped
string for an experiment that never happened. It now refuses and points at the collapse warning.

add the sample-efficiency half of continual learning, and a test for DISCOVERY
LEARNING CURVE (in-report). Retention asks whether old material survives. Nothing asked how fast new
material is picked UP, which is the other half. Held-out bits/byte per process on the RATE_EVERY
cadence, with each process marked active or absent at the time of measurement, so both transitions
are visible -- the step a process ENTERS the stream and the step it FADES.

  step:          150     300     450
  process 0:   4.79A   4.67.   4.40.
  process 2:   7.60.   3.53A   3.57A
  mean change per 150 steps while ACTIVE  +0.902 b/B  (learning)
  mean change per 150 steps while ABSENT  +0.192 b/B  (still improving, via shared structure)

Process 2 sits at 7.60 while it has never been seen and drops to 3.53 the step it enters. Absent
processes still improve slightly rather than decaying, which is a better result than the eviction
numbers had led me to expect and is now visible instead of inferred.

Two bugs found building it, both mine and both of the kind this project keeps producing:
 - the sampler was wrapped in `except Exception: model.train()`, which swallowed the error and printed
   NOTHING AT ALL -- the section simply never appeared. It now reports the failure once.
 - what it was swallowing: nbytes() reads BLEN, which is None until the final re-tokenization whenever
   TOK_ONLINE is set, so it is unusable mid-run. The byte denominator now comes from the live
   tokenizer, cached per vocab size since the vocabulary grows underneath it.

STABILITY (probe_stability.py). The clustering scores compare a partition to categories we spliced
in, so a high score means "it reconstructed our scaffold". Agreement with an INDEPENDENT RUN means
something stronger. Each run is treated as a labelling function over window-space -- encode, take the
nearest domain centroid -- so two runs are comparable despite different streams, different domain ids
and different domain COUNTS. Both are applied to one common probe set.

With a null, because raw agreement between few-large-cluster partitions is high by accident: the same
agreement against a random relabelling of B that preserves B's cluster sizes exactly.

  two 80 kB runs differing only in SEED, 11 and 10 domains:
    agreement A vs B                 0.568
    shuffled-B floor                 0.015   [0.012-0.019 over 20 draws]
    EXCESS                          +0.552
    agreement with seeded corpora    A 0.646 | B 0.676

Two runs sharing nothing but the corpora found substantially the same partition. That is the first
evidence in this project that the structure is in the data rather than in the initialisation, and it
is the one form of it the seeded labels are incapable of providing.

  fitted on TRAIN, scored on held-out:  uniform 5.185 | order-0 4.642 | order-1 3.568 | MODEL 4.267
  >> DOES NOT BEAT ORDER-1 (-0.699) -- a two-line frequency table does as well

with what is already here -- encode successive windows of the CONTINUATION and ask which TRUE-corpus
centroid each is nearest. Bracketed both ways, because the bare fraction means nothing:

  model ALONE 0.50 | model+MEMORY 0.50 | REAL text (ceiling) 0.85 | chance (floor) 0.25
  >> PARTIAL -- better than chance but wanders well before real text does; memory is neutral

Real text scores 0.85 rather than 1.00 because the encoder is imperfect, which is precisely why the
ceiling has to be measured rather than assumed. Sitting halfway between floor and ceiling is a fair
description of what the samples look like by eye, and now it is a number that can move.

Still missing and not built here: stability of the partition across seeds (needs two runs, so it is a
harness rather than a report section), and per-process learning curves after a process ENTERS, which
PHASED now makes measurable.

a5ac033a16a4609a4c95407fa03de592e7a90ba7
2026-07-28
non-stationary by default, and fix the two things that kept it from being usable
Asked why PHASED was off: there is no reason. It shipped in the first commit ("Add overarching
continual-learning package"), defaulted to 0 alongside the ablation flags, and was never once turned
on -- by me or by anyone. Same failure class as MANAGE_MERGE=0.12, the BATCH_W cadences and
SEG_MIN/WIN: a default that silently decided what the experiment was.

It is also the wrong default on the merits. A stationary i.i.d. splice of N corpora does not require
continual learning at all; nothing has to survive a distribution shift because there is no shift. It
is ordinary training with extra machinery, and it is what every number in this project was measured
on. PHASED=1 is now the default and PHASED=0 is the deliberate ablation, with a startup warning
saying in as many words that the run does not test continual learning.

Two bugs had to be fixed first, both of which would have made the flag useless to the person who
finally set it:

1. PH_BOUNDS was APPENDED to inside build_stream, which runs once per epoch under DISK_STREAM. The
   current phase is read as `sum(1 for b in PH_BOUNDS if bpos >= b) - 1`, so with 4 entries added per
   epoch that index reached 8 by epoch 3 for a position whose phase was 2 -- past the end of
   PHASE_SCHED. PHASED=1 would have failed in exactly the multi-epoch configuration it exists for.
   Now cleared and rebuilt each epoch; verified over 3 epochs, phases cycle 0-3, 0-3 cleanly.

2. The RETENTION metric added in the previous commit compared the first fifth of the stream against
   the last fifth and asserted they were "statistically identical". True only when stationary. Under
   PHASED phase 0 is [0,1] and phase 3 is [2,3] -- an EMPTY intersection -- so it was measuring which
   corpora are intrinsically harder, the very confound that had to be corrected by hand when the
   non-stationary test was first run. It is now conditioned on the process label: each process's
   earliest windows against its own latest windows, same material either side.

Measured, 80 kB, 4 corpora:
  PHASED=1  p1 -0.203  p2 +0.048  p3 +0.226   mean +0.024  RETAINED
  PHASED=0  p0 +0.025  p1 -0.048  p2 +0.049  p3 +0.561  mean +0.147  DRIFTING
The stationary run reports the WORSE retention, which is the sort of inversion the old whole-stream
version could not have surfaced. Both are single runs at small scale; the point of the change is that
the number now means what it says.

2026-07-27
measure retention -- the continual-learning claim rested on a test that had never been run
AUDIT FINDING. The report has fourteen sections. Exactly one of them bears on catastrophic
forgetting, the defining problem of continual learning: the NON-STATIONARY block behind PHASED=1.
PHASED defaults to 0 and it had never been executed in any run of this project. Everything we have
been tuning -- purity, homogeneity, completeness, V-measure, fragmentation, silhouette -- scores the
ORGANISATION of a store, against categories we ourselves spliced in, and none of it says whether the
system keeps what it learns.

Ran it. Processes enter and fade over four phases; faded = [0,1], active = [2,3]:

  faded  4.819 b/B     active 3.411 b/B     memory surviving: p0=0  p1=0  p2=2198  p3=4778
  "UNLEARN a FADED process 0: SKIPPED -- only 0 entries left (evicted); test would be vacuous"

Corpora differ in difficulty, so that comparison is confounded. Against a matched STATIONARY control
(same config, PHASED=0, per-process 3.811 / 4.520 / 3.639 / 4.303):

  faded-set baseline 4.166 -> 4.819   FORGETTING +0.65 bits/byte
  active-set baseline 3.971 -> 3.411  IMPROVED  -0.56 (they monopolised the later stream)

Two things follow. The system forgets substantially. And every "unlearning is surgical and local"
result this project has reported was measured on ACTIVE material only -- deleting what the bounded
store has already evicted is a no-op, which is why the faded arm of that test skips itself.

So retention is now measured on EVERY run, with no flag and no labels: bits/byte on the first fifth
of the stream versus the last fifth. The stream is a splice of the same corpora throughout, so the
two are statistically identical, and BOTH were trained on -- a gap is forgetting, not generalisation.
Memory is included, because retention is a property of weights plus store and the store evicts.

First measurement on a default 80 kB run: first fifth 4.106, last fifth 3.873, forgetting +0.233 b/B
-- DRIFTING. Not catastrophic at this scale, and not zero.

The line says outright that this is the metric the continual-learning claim rests on and the domain
scores are not.

8914dd17aa95074684e6ad74f70c58547d96844a
2026-07-27
domains: measure whether the partition means anything, without the seeded labels
Every clustering score in this report is computed against the corpora WE spliced in, so at best it
measures reconstruction of our own categories -- it cannot say whether the system discovered
anything. Two changes.

1. MANAGE_MERGE back to 0.28 from 0.45. 0.45 maximised V-measure against those four seeded corpora,
   which is the wrong target, and it was bought with the one property the domain id actually
   controls. `did` is consumed in exactly three places: mem.src (provenance -> delete_src /
   reassign_src), dom_exp (reporting), and the clustering report. ROUTING DOES NOT USE IT -- fabric
   and experts route on the continuous `gist`. So the domain COUNT barely affects prediction; what it
   sets is the GRANULARITY OF FORGETTING. Measured, unlearning one process: 25 domains -> 20 deletes
   of ~1.6% each; 4 domains -> a single delete of 30%. Coarser domains do not predict better, they
   only make editing blunter. 0.28 is MERGE_FRAC*NEW_DIST, the value the code was designed around.
   This is a POLICY knob (how finely do you want to be able to forget?), not a correctness one.

2. A label-free test of whether the partition is informative: restrict retrieval to the query's OWN
   domain and compare against a RANDOM OTHER domain, at matched restriction. Deliberately not
   own-vs-global, which global should win and which measures something else.

   WITH ITS NULL, which is the part that matters. "Own domain" is the domain of the query's nearest
   entry, so own-domain retrieval always contains the global top-1 hit and foreign never does -- it
   wins on a partition made of coin flips. The identical comparison is therefore re-run on a random
   PERMUTATION of the provenance tags: same sizes, same top-1 advantage, no information. Only the
   excess over that floor is evidence.

   It changes the verdict on the first thing it was pointed at:
     4 corpora   own 4.167 vs foreign 4.527 = +0.360  |  null +0.265  |  EXCESS +0.095  informative
     eng alone   own 3.635 vs foreign 3.920 = +0.286  |  null +0.341  |  EXCESS -0.055  NOT
   The raw English gap of +0.286 looks convincing and is below chance. Without the control this would
   have been reported as "English sub-domains carry information", and it would have been wrong.

   Caveats stated where it prints: n=1, 60 kB, small model, and it measures PREDICTIVE utility only --
   domains can still earn their keep for EDITING while failing this.

13e787ac421bae8dec022cb61bb4c1dcdf3a2237
2026-07-27
domains: the consolidation scale was 3x tighter than the creation scale, and had been since the start
manage() computes `md = merge_dist if merge_dist > 0 else MERGE_FRAC*NEW_DIST`. MANAGE_MERGE defaulted
to 0.12, which is non-zero, so it OVERRODE the fallback -- and the 0.28 that MERGE_FRAC*NEW_DIST was
written to produce, under a comment reading "ONE scale for create AND consolidate", had never once
run. Creation used 0.35 while consolidation used 0.12. Domains could be created three times more
readily than they could be joined, which is the whole of the fragmentation this project has spent
weeks attributing to the assign rule, the encoder, and the creation threshold in turn.

Measured, 4 MB GPU run, long segments, everything else held fixed:

  MANAGE_MERGE   live   purity   homogeneity   completeness     V     fragmentation
      0.12        25     0.97       0.90          0.60         0.72        8x
      0.45         4     0.97       0.89          0.89         0.89        1x

Four live domains for four seeded corpora, a clean bijection ([(0,3),(1,2),(4,1),(5,0)]), every one
recurrent, none visited once, and purity/homogeneity UNCHANGED from the 25-domain run -- so this is
consolidation of siblings, not corpora being smeared together.

The falsification test is the part worth keeping. Pushing the threshold further, CPU, same config:

  0.45 -> 7 live, purity 0.96, hom 0.88        0.80 -> 4 live, purity 0.71, hom 0.60   COUNTERFEIT 4
  0.60 -> 6 live, purity 0.88, hom 0.78        1.00 -> 5 live, purity 0.60, hom 0.52

Past 0.45 the count keeps falling while purity collapses. "4 domains" is reachable two ways and the
COUNT ALONE CANNOT DISTINGUISH THEM. Also: 0.45 gives 7 on CPU and 4 on GPU, so the threshold is a
scale, not a target -- the count is a property of the data, which is what makes the GPU result a
finding rather than a fit.

Left undone on purpose: the natural consolidation scale is the MEASURED radius (pooled 0.29-0.62
across these runs), not a constant -- two domains should merge when their acceptance balls
substantially overlap. That is a design change and it is unmeasured, so it is documented, not shipped.

97acf0575d2cfe90cddb07d16da346f35a793a60
2026-07-27
signature: make what the encoder reads a measurable choice, and let it grow with the vocabulary
Three knobs, all defaulting to the historical behaviour so nothing changes unless asked.

SIG_WIN -- byte width of the signature window. It was WIN, a byte count, against a loop stride of WIN
TOKENS, so the encoder saw WIN/(WIN*bytes_per_token) of the stream: ~53% at 1.9 B/token in the 4 MB
run, and the fraction SHRINKS as compression improves. Nobody chose that. A [signature] line now
reports width, stride and coverage at startup, and names the SIG_WIN that would cover it all.

SIG_SPACE=tokens -- read the LM's token window instead of raw bytes, WITHOUT freezing anything.
Byte-level was a stability choice: with TOK_ONLINE the vocabulary grows mid-run and _retok
re-segments the stream, while domain centroids persist for the whole run and memory provenance is
keyed by domain id. But DynamicTokenizer minting is APPEND-ONLY -- an id never changes meaning, only
the SEGMENTATION of text changes -- and rekey() already re-encodes every reservoir on a cadence,
which is precisely the mechanism for tracking that drift. New ids are warm-started in the ENCODER
from their two constituents, the trick the LM already used at WARMSTART; the encoder needs it more,
because a centroid is a mean of encodings and one freshly-random id inside a window perturbs every
signature containing it, which the assembler reads as a domain shift. The signature space grows with
the vocabulary rather than being pinned to a snapshot of it.

Consequences wired up: ENC_SEQ follows SIG_SPACE (contrastive_step must TRAIN in the space the
signature is READ in), retok re-points ENC_SEQ when it rebuilds the token stream, `seen` is passed as
an index into ENC_SEQ (bpos counts bytes, i counts tokens), ENC_V sizes to the vocabulary in token
space, and the lookahead builder uses the same width as `ew` instead of hard-coding WIN.

Measured, CPU, 4 corpora, 60 kB, long segments (n=1 each -- weak, treat as direction only):
  space=bytes  W=128  coverage  67%   11 live   purity 0.96  hom 0.89  V 0.74
  space=bytes  W=256  coverage 100%    8 live   purity 0.86  hom 0.77  V 0.67
  space=TOKENS W=128                   7 live   purity 0.89  hom 0.80  V 0.73
Closing the coverage gap made it WORSE, which was not the expected direction: at 100% coverage the
window (256 B) exceeds the stride (192 B) so windows OVERLAP, and an overlapping window smears
material from both sides of a boundary into one signature. Non-overlapping sampling gives crisper
boundaries than complete coverage does. Token space roughly ties with the default at a third fewer
domains. Defaults unchanged on this evidence.

7ca2061ba10b97570f05575064487239ce8d2e1e
2026-07-27
fix: SAVE_CKPT=0 wrote checkpoints to a directory named `0`, and it got committed
Every other switch in this file is an integer flag, so SAVE_CKPT=0 is the obvious way to turn
checkpointing off -- but SAVE_CKPT is a PATH, and "0" is a truthy string in Python. `if not ck:
return` never fired, os.makedirs("0") ran, and runs wrote ckpt.pt / ckpt.prev.pt / source.bin into a
directory literally named `0` in the repo root.

.gitignore did not catch it: source.bin is not *.pt, and `0/` is not `runs/`. So `git add -A` picked
it up and it was committed and pushed -- 3.7 MB of checkpoint plus a 60 kB source.bin that showed up
in the last pull as "create mode 100644 0/source.bin". My doing, from using SAVE_CKPT=0 in the CPU
test runs.

Three parts: normalise the disabled spellings ("0", "", "off", "no", "none", "false") to an absent
SAVE_CKPT once, before any of the four call sites read it; remove `0/` from the index and the tree;
and add /0/ and source.bin to .gitignore so an old working tree stays clean.

Verified: SAVE_CKPT=0, =off and ="" each create no directory at all, and a real path
(SAVE_CKPT=<dir>/ck.pt) still writes ck.pt as before.

2026-07-27
guard: warn when a splice segment is only a few analysis windows long
probe_ckpt_geometry.py on the 4 MB rerun's own encoder settles the question the genuineness report
could not: mean TRUE-corpus silhouette +0.24 (eng +0.26, py +0.17, num +0.37, c +0.15), 1-NN corpus
accuracy 0.984, d_between/d_within 1.71. The encoder separates the kinds. It is not the bottleneck,
and the earlier "encoder budget dominates the assign rule" reading is withdrawn.

Nor is it the creation rule. What the run is actually up against is arithmetic in the testbed:

  3213 true switches over 4,000,000 bytes  ->  1245 bytes per splice segment
  WIN=256 TOKENS at ~1.9 bytes/token       ->  ~486 bytes per analysis window
  => a segment is 2.6 windows long, and SUSTAIN=2 of those are spent DETECTING the boundary.

Under one settled window per segment survives. SEG_MIN/SEG_MAX (700/1800) were chosen when WIN was
~96 BYTES -- 13 windows per segment -- and never revisited when WIN became 256 tokens.

Falsified two-sidedly on CPU, everything else identical (d64, WIN=128 bytes, 4 corpora, 60 kB,

  segment      windows/seg   live   purity   homogeneity   V
  180-460 B        2.5        15     0.54       0.25      0.19
  700-1800 B       9.8        16     0.87       0.70      0.50
  2800-7200 B     39.0        12     0.88       0.80      0.68

V is monotone in segment length while the LIVE COUNT barely moves (15/16/12) -- so this is not a
fragmentation effect, it is how much settled material a segment contains. The 2.5-window arm
reproduces the GPU run (V 0.19 / purity 0.54 there, V 0.30 / purity 0.66 at 2.6 windows).

The guard computes segment/window from SEG_MIN, SEG_MAX, WIN and the live bytes-per-token, and says
how many windows remain after SUSTAIN. It fires on the 4 MB configuration (3.3 windows, 1.3 left) and
stays silent at WIN=96 bytes and at raised SEG_MIN/SEG_MAX. probe_ckpt_geometry.py's verdict now
points at this warning instead of naming the creation rule.

ab3a311fff7a0e2b52ed85c78b2b9d0581a41c32
2026-07-27
retract the COLLAPSE CHECK verdict; add the probe that can actually settle it
I shipped a check last commit that compared median centroid separation against a random-unit-vector
null and printed "signature space is COLLAPSED -- fix the ENCODER, not the assign rule" below -3
sigma. On the 4 MB rerun it fired at -5.2 sigma. It is wrong, and it took one measurement to show it:

  probe_ckpt_geometry.py on a checkpoint whose encoder is measurably HEALTHY -- true-corpus
  silhouette +0.25, 1-NN corpus accuracy 0.90, d_between/d_within 2.68 -- scores -4.8 sigma.

-4.8 for a good encoder against -5.2 for the one under suspicion. The test could not separate the two
cases it existed to separate. Centroids of related text are nowhere near orthogonal, so the null was
never the right one. Retracted: the line now reports the numbers and says plainly that it cannot
attribute them.

What replaces it is scale-free and needs no null: the MEDIAN SILHOUETTE compares separation against a
domain's own scatter. It also discriminates in practice -- +0.62 on a healthy run, +0.00 on the rerun.

But neither number can settle the real question, because BOTH are computed between the centroids the
assembler PRODUCED: a fragmented population is crowded by construction, whatever the encoder did. Only
the true labels can separate "the space is poor" from "the population is fragmented".

probe_ckpt_geometry.py does that. It loads the encoder a run actually trained (sized from the
checkpoint, so it reads old and new checkpoints alike), samples windows of each true corpus, and
reports per-corpus cohesion, nearest-centroid separation, silhouette, d_within/d_between, and 1-NN
corpus accuracy. Labels are used only to score; nothing trains. It renders one of two verdicts:
separable -> fix the CREATION rule; not separable -> fix the ENCODER.

It also prints 1-NN alongside silhouette because those genuinely disagree -- kind can be recoverable
by nearest neighbour while centroids sit close -- and conflating them is how the last three encoder
conclusions in this project went wrong.

5218756a66106a4ab8838866c87588e1ace45b8a
2026-07-27
perf: size the signature encoder by the stream it reads, not by the LM's vocab
SigEncoder allocated nn.Embedding(V, d), and V is VMAX in online-tokenizer mode -- but ENC_SEQ is
the raw BYTE stream there (_resample's ONLINE branch returns `_b`), so ids 256..VMAX-1 could never be
indexed. At the 4 MB run's VMAX=16384 / d=768 that is 12.4M of the encoder's 16.2M parameters, 77%,
unreachable yet allocated, held in two AdamW moment buffers and traversed by the optimizer every
step -- while the encoder is 70% of wall clock.

It is NOT unconditionally 256: with TOKENIZER=1 TOK_ONLINE=0 the corpora themselves are tokenized
and ENC_SEQ really does carry ids up to TOK.vocab_size. ENC_V picks by mode.

Measured in isolation at the 4 MB run's geometry (768-d rows, only ids 0-255 ever indexed, dense
grad, one Adam step):
  Embedding(16384, 768)   12.58M params   137.3 ms   151.0 MB weights + Adam moments
  Embedding(  256, 768)    0.20M params    53.8 ms     2.4 MB
2.6x on that term and 148 MB of memory traffic per step that was pure waste. This does not touch what
the encoder LEARNS: the removed rows were never reachable, so the forward pass is unchanged.

Restore paths: _load_enc() slices an over-sized saved embedding down to ENC_V (rows 0..ENC_V-1 hold
all the training that ever happened) and says so. prompt.py sizes its own SigEncoder from the
checkpoint tensor instead of guessing, so it loads old and new checkpoints alike.

Verified end to end with TOKENIZER=1 TOK_ONLINE=1 VMAX=4096: the checkpoint holds encoder embedding
(256, 64) alongside LM embedding (4096, 64) -- encoder sized to bytes, LM keeping the full vocab --
and RESUME from it restores cleanly (step 89, 2000 memory entries, 8 domains).

2cffa47aa91c22a5c83b33ab4b33b2d9055ca96e
2026-07-27
report: separation was measured with an order statistic that shrinks as the population grows
Two defects in the domain-genuineness block, both of which made the 4 MB run look worse than it is.

1. `sizes` was built from `by`, i.e. from `assigns` -- so while assigns.append sat below the batch
   accumulator, every size in the table read 1/BATCH_W of the truth. The 4 MB BATCH_W=16 run printed
   "size 134" for a domain of roughly 2100 windows, and the size>=20 half of the GENUINE test was
   16x too strict. It now reads asm.size, incremented inside update() on every window
   unconditionally, so it cannot drift from the stream again.

2. `sep` is a MIN over the other N-1 centroids -- an extreme order statistic that shrinks
   mechanically as the population grows, so it penalises exactly the fragmentation the recurrence
   fold exists to reduce. For OVERLAPPING self-assembled domains, which is the stated design intent,
   "is anything nearby" is also the wrong question: neighbouring domains are supposed to touch. The
   report now prints the MEDIAN separation alongside the min, and a silhouette against each.

Read together they separate two failure modes that the min alone conflates: sil<0 with median sil>0
means crowded by a near neighbour but globally placed (fragmentation, which merging fixes); both
negative means the signature space has no cluster structure at all and no assign rule can help.

Added a COLLAPSE CHECK on the same line: random unit vectors in SIG_D dimensions sit at cosine
distance 1.0 +/- 1/sqrt(SIG_D), so the median centroid separation can be reported in sigma against
that null. It says outright whether to fix the encoder or the assign rule.

Measured, 60 kB / 4 corpora / d64 / WIN=128 / ENC_WARMUP=4000: mean cohesion 0.74, separation
0.34 nearest / 0.80 median, silhouette +0.08 nearest / +0.54 median, collapse check -1.6 sigma
("centroids span the space"). Median silhouette is POSITIVE for every one of the 16 domains. The
4 MB run at ENC_WARMUP=30000 reported cohesion 0.57 and nearest separation 0.18 -- internal scatter
0.43 against 0.26 here. Those two runs differ in d, WIN and stream length as well as warmup, so this
is not a clean attribution on its own; it agrees in direction with sweep stage 1, where warmup2000
(V 0.60, homogeneity 0.80) beats warmup30000 (V 0.58, 0.77) and warmup8000 (V 0.47, 0.62) with
everything else held fixed.

Verified: the rest of the run is byte-identical to the pre-change log.

5a72970bd000b1cc3fe60551a8d15cfecbfeece7
2026-07-26
report: the adaptive warmup claimed a plateau it never detected
`_wfloor = min(ENC_WARMUP_MIN, wu)` and the plateau test is `t >= _wfloor`, but the loop runs
`for t in range(wu)` and so never reaches t == wu. Setting ENC_WARMUP_MIN == ENC_WARMUP therefore
makes the early stop UNREACHABLE -- the one setting that silently disables the whole feature. The
message printed "stopped at N/N on separation plateau" unconditionally, so the 4 MB GH200 run, which
set both to 30000 and consequently paid all 30000 contrastive steps, was told it had converged.

Now says which of the two actually happened, and warns explicitly when MIN >= budget. Verified both
branches: MIN=600/budget=600 -> "ran the FULL budget ... no plateau detected" plus the warning;
MIN=100/budget=600 -> "STOPPED EARLY at 101/600 on separation plateau".

c8ba6357f9636a3a6efc1189f0064e55fe790b68
2026-07-26
fix: four cadences below the batch accumulator never fired when BATCH_W > 1
The main loop accumulates BATCH_W windows before the LM step:

    _bx.append(...)
    if len(_bx) < BATCH_W: i += WIN; step += 1; continue

`step` advances on EVERY window, but everything after that line executes only on FLUSH steps, which
land on a fixed residue mod BATCH_W. So `step % N == 0` below it asks for a simultaneous solution to
two congruences that usually has none, and the block silently never runs. Simulated over 200k windows:

    BATCH_W    windows recorded in `assigns`   mint events   retok events
       1                  100%                     999            66
       2                   50%                       0             0
      15                    6.7%                     0             0
      16                    6.2%                     0             0
      32                    3.1%                     0             0

Zero for EVERY BATCH_W > 1 tested, odd ones included -- not just the even case.

Four things sat below that line:
  - GROW_EVERY  (online tokenizer minting)
  - RETOK_EVERY (re-tokenization with the grown vocab)
  - CKPT_EVERY  (periodic checkpoint -- a multi-day run would never have saved one)
  - the LM loss-curve sample
plus two PER-WINDOW statements that describe the window, not the batch:
  - `assigns.append(...)`, which every clustering metric is computed from
  - the tokenizer's live pair tally

Observed, not theorised. The 4 MB BATCH_W=16 GH200 run printed
  "[tokenizer @ 6000] vocab 512/16384 (minting live; +0 since last retok)"
and finished at vocab 512 -- a model sized for 16384 ids running on the 512 the SEED passes had
already produced, with live minting and re-tokenization both dead. That same run reported two
recurrence figures that disagreed by 6x (assembler counter: top domain 598 entries; report block:
100 visits) because `assigns` held one window in sixteen, and recurrence counts maximal consecutive
runs, which subsampling destroys. Its purity/homogeneity/completeness/V-measure were all computed
from 6.2% of the stream.

Fix: per-window bookkeeping moves above the accumulator; every cadence below it becomes
elapsed-since-last-fire, which is phase-independent and resume-safe. The signature lookahead horizon
tracks the same threshold, so queued windows cannot outlive the stream rebuild they were built from.

Verified: at BATCH_W=16 live minting now runs (512 -> 536 where it was 0), and the assembler's
per-domain entry counts and the report's visit counts now agree ([9,8,5,5,3,3,2,2] vs
[8,8,5,5,3,3,2,2]) where they diverged 6x before. At the shipped BATCH_W=1 default the whole run is
byte-identical, so this is a no-op there and a correctness fix above it.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

510c69517424fe7926070f471b66a365ec19fd3b
2026-07-25
domains: the encoder budget was the dominant term, and management was never running
Two findings from the first end-to-end A/B of the radius+fold work on real text. Both are things
the previous numbers could not have shown, because both were disabled by arithmetic.

1. manage() NEVER RAN. Domain management shared MANAGE_EVERY=500 with the expert and world-model
   populations. A 60 kB run is 468 steps, so `step % 500 == 0` was never true and merge, cull and
   the new fold all executed ZERO times; the 120 kB GH200 runs are 937 steps, so it fired ONCE.
   Every domain-population figure this project has reported -- including the 142-domain run -- was
   produced with the consolidation half of the mechanism switched off. Domain management now has
   its own cadence (DOM_MANAGE_EVERY=100); the expert and world cadences are untouched, since
   their costs and grace periods are tuned to 500.

2. THE ASSIGN RULE WAS NOT THE MAIN PROBLEM. With management actually firing, on real text
   (60 kB, 4 corpora, ENC_WARMUP=4000, one variable at a time):

     arm                        live  created  folded  recurrent  bnd prec/rec  hom   comp    V
     constants (old default)     50     50       0        34%      0.61/0.84   0.80  0.29   0.42
     radius+fold                 36     46      10        61%      0.61/0.84   0.70  0.28   0.40
     floor K=8 alone             23     28       0        48%      0.78/0.84   0.70  0.38   0.49
     floor K=8 + radius+fold     16     21       1        88%      0.76/0.86   0.70  0.39   0.50
     floor K=4 + radius+fold      6     10       0        83%      0.79/0.59   0.56  0.52   0.54

   Radius+fold alone consolidates and roughly doubles the recurrent share, but leaves V flat and
   costs homogeneity. The ENCODER LOSS FLOOR dominates: it alone takes 50 -> 23 and V 0.42 -> 0.49,
   and composed with radius+fold gives 16 domains of which 88% recur -- the first configuration to
   beat the V=0.42 that four earlier attempts all lost to. ENC_FLOOR_K is now ON by default at 8.

   ln(1 + (B-1)/K) is the loss of an encoder that cannot separate the positive from K-1 equally
   good candidates. If the stream holds NP kinds, ~(B-1)/NP of the negatives are the same kind as
   the positive, so a perfect KIND encoder floors at K = NP -- and K=4 (= NP here) does land closest
   to the truth, 6 domains for 4 corpora. The default is 8 and not 4 anyway: K=4 buys its V by
   finding only 38 of 49 true switches and by letting homogeneity fall to 0.56, and a domain that
   blends two corpora poisons provenance -- delete_src would unlearn the wrong material.

   Stated plainly: one run per arm, one stream length, one seed. sweep_domains.sh stage 1b grids K
   from 0 to 32 at 120 kB, and stage 3 now grids DOM_MANAGE_EVERY with 500 reproducing the old
   never-fires behaviour.

Also: the isolated synthetic result (64 -> 4 live, V 1.00) does NOT transfer to real text as-is,
and the sweep now says so where it would otherwise be read as a prediction.

f0375c592a1636a1c544f5426f4eb35af9ccedea
2026-07-25
domains: measure the acceptance radius instead of assuming it, and fold what never recurs
The domain population was EXTENSIVE -- it grew with the number of bytes consumed rather than
converging on the number of kinds of material present. Measured on a controlled test that isolates
the assembler from the encoder (synthetic signatures, 4 recurring processes, known truth, 3 seeds):

                              live (truth 4)   V      @ 120 / 240 / 480 segments
  constant thresholds              64.0       0.82      64 -> 116 -> 193   GROWS
  + measured radius x1.2           18.0       0.95      18 ->  20 ->  25   nearly flat
  + recurrence fold                 4.0       1.00       4 ->   4 ->   4   exact

Two mechanisms:

RADIUS FROM THE RESERVOIR. Every acceptance rule so far compared the assign distance against a
number never measured on that domain -- NEW_DIST is a constant, the margin is a ratio to whatever
happens to be second-nearest -- while the metric's own scale inflates as the encoder trains
(d to own centroid runs .037 -> .668 over 200-4000 steps). The radius is now the DOM_RQ quantile of
d(reservoir window, own centroid), computed inside rekey where those windows are already encoded,
so it costs nothing and re-measures itself every time the centroids move.

This is the second attempt. The first estimated the radius from the distances at which a domain was
MATCHED, which cannot bootstrap: matching needs a radius, so nothing matched, no samples
accumulated, and the radius never activated -- 0 of 143 domains ever learned one, and a pooled
prior over the same censored sample did not fix it (the pool held 3-5 entries). The reservoir is
uncensored: a window is in it because it was assigned, whatever the threshold said.

RECURRENCE FOLD. Domains are created at boundaries and nothing ever asked whether the thing created
came back. A corpus recurs; a splice segment does not. Domains under DOM_MIN_VISITS separate
entries, after DOM_RECUR_HORIZON boundaries of life, fold into their nearest neighbour -- folded,
not deleted, so memory provenance follows and the survivor's reservoir gains a second segment.
Bounded by DOM_FOLD_MULT x the pooled radius, and skipped entirely before the first rekey; an
unguarded fold collapses the population to one domain.

DOM_RCAP was calibrated rather than assumed. The value first tried, 0.5, was the worst in the table
(65 live / V 0.82 -- it strangles the radius back to the baseline it exists to fix, because the cap
is set by a SAME-corpus sibling). 0 and >= 1.5 are indistinguishable; the default is 2.0, free in
the healthy regime and still a bound on a runaway radius.

Also:
- recurrence state (visits, per-domain birth boundary, boundary clock, radii) now survives RESUME.
  Without it every restored domain resumed at visits=0 against a clock restarting at 0, so the fold
  would swallow the assembled history DOM_RECUR_HORIZON boundaries after any resume.
- merge and fold share one _absorb(): provenance, reservoir pooling and id resolution in one place.
- ENC_FLOOR_K (off): gate the encoder STEP below ln(1+(B-1)/K). Training to convergence is
  measurably harmful -- 1-NN corpus accuracy peaks at 1000-4000 steps and degrades after -- but
  freezing is not continual-safe. The gate resumes by itself when new material raises the loss.
- ENC_PROTO (off): draw a fraction of InfoNCE pairs from one domain's reservoir, so the encoder is
  trained on the question the assembler actually asks. Self-referential and therefore swept, not
  adopted; bounded to a fraction of the batch so the rest stays grounded in stream locality.
- both were already referenced by sweep_domains.sh, whose unread-knob guard was aborting the sweep.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01PksNp6ECrYm34zjV2rbm4x

efb818aa64d6000e796b0372cff09c76c52cc2d8
2026-07-25
metric: judge domains on RECURRENCE, not on recovering the seeded corpora
The four corpora are a SCAFFOLD -- they are how the stream is built, not
what the system is asked to find. The design intent is self-assembled,
naturally OVERLAPPING domains, so "did you recover exactly 4" is the wrong
question, and V-measure against 4 labels actively PENALISES the intended
behaviour: discovering that English splits into narrative and dialogue, or
that Python and C share a code-like region, lowers V while being exactly
what is wanted. I had inherited the seeded labels as the optimisation
target and spent four attempts tuning toward them.

What actually separates a genuine self-assembled domain from a
splice-segment artifact is whether it is RE-ENTERED. Real structure recurs
when similar material returns; an artifact is visited once and never again.
The report now prints, as the headline: visits per domain (a visit being a
maximal run of consecutive windows assigned to it), the fraction visited
exactly ONCE, and the fraction recurring >=3 times. The seeded-corpus
numbers are kept but relabelled as a scaffold comparison.

This also explains why the seeded metrics contradicted each other all
session: purity RISES with fragmentation, completeness FALLS with it, and
neither can see whether anything recurs. The measured 96 domains against
89-96 splice segments, at a mean of 9.5 windows each, is a near-exact
one-to-one map onto segments -- many, disjoint, single-visit domains, which
is the OPPOSITE failure from the few, overlapping, recurring ones the
thesis predicts. Only recurrence distinguishes them.

Consequence: the four configurations measured so far need re-ranking on
recurrence. Config A scored best on V-measure, but if its 142 domains are
all single-visit while another produces 30 that recur, A is the worse
system by the criterion that matches the design.

Co-Authored-By: Claude <noreply@anthropic.com>
Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01PksNp6ECrYm34zjV2rbm4x

  A fixed NEW_DIST/SHIFT_DIST        30000      142    0.96       0.42   <- best

off, until a sweep shows one beating V=0.42. The scale analysis behind them

radius from the distances at which IT was matched, so the test is "within
THIS domain's habitual spread" rather than one number for all domains at
all times. That addresses the global drift and per-domain variation
together, which neither a global constant nor a pure ratio does.

sweep_domains.sh now refuses to start if any knob it sets is read by
nothing. Eight of its thirty-six were unread -- the D_MODEL_B failure
again, and a sweep is the worst place for it because each unread knob turns
a stage into duplicate rows that read as a clean null result. Five remain
unimplemented (DOM_RECUR, DOM_RECUR_HORIZON, DOM_MIN_VISITS, ENC_FLOOR_K,
ENC_PROTO); the guard names them and aborts.

d6acf203d8cd833b07abe425aa0a0746f4ce73a2
2026-07-25
fix: scale-free assignment AND boundary detection, validated against the signature probe
The GH200 signature probe settles the diagnosis: this was never an encoder
problem. 4-way 1-NN corpus accuracy is 91-99% at every trained state and
every positive radius (chance 25%), and even the UNTRAINED bigram control
scores AUC 0.889. The signature ranks corpora correctly throughout.

What breaks is that the metric's SCALE inflates as the encoder trains while
every threshold is a constant. Measured d(query, own centroid) at R=2:

  ENC_POS_MAX=2*WIN   N=200 0.031 | N=1000 0.223 | N=4000 0.419 | N=16000 0.569
  NEW_DIST                                        0.35 (constant)

so re-entry re-identifies early in training and SPAWNS later -- SPAWN% goes
0.0% -> 24.8% -> 56.9% -> 72.5% while 1-NN accuracy stays 91-97%. That is
the signature being right and the threshold being wrong.

The relative margin is invariant to the whole inflation. Validated against
every probe cell: d_own/d_other stays in 0.10-0.57 for every TRAINED state
at every ENC_POS_MAX, and exceeds DOM_MARGIN=0.75 only at N=0 where the
encoder is untrained and spawning is correct. One constant works everywhere.

The boundary detector has the identical disease and now gets the identical
treatment: within-segment adjacent-window distance runs 0.044 -> 0.229 ->
0.317 -> 0.340 against a constant SHIFT_DIST=0.30, so boundary precision
falls from 0.92 at N=200 to 0.27 at N=16000 -- tripping on ordinary
within-segment variation. SHIFT_REL compares against a running quantile of
recent adjacent distances instead.

Also replaces the previous adaptive-threshold attempt, which tracked the
median of distances AT WHICH ASSIGNMENT HAPPENED -- a censored sample that
structurally cannot follow the drift it existed to follow.

Two further probe findings, not yet acted on: 1-NN accuracy PEAKS around
N=1000-4000 and DEGRADES with more training (posmax4: 98.5% at N=1000 ->
80.4% at N=16000), so ENC_WARMUP=30000 is well past the optimum; and stale
centroids inflate spawning (SPAWN 24.8% -> 64.6% at rekey lag 800).

380012944ffbada589600af7ea23a9d718718396
2026-07-25
fix: ENC_POS_MAX above its default crashed contrastive_step; probe_signature bug workaround
The knob I added to test wider InfoNCE positives could not be used at any
non-default value. contrastive_step bounded the anchor with
`hi = seen - 3*WIN`, which only leaves room for a positive at the DEFAULT
radius (off <= 2*WIN, plus WIN for the window itself). With ENC_POS_MAX >
2*WIN the positive ran past the end of the stream -- IndexError on the
gather path, a short window into torch.tensor on the list path. In a real
run it would survive while the read position was far from the end and then
crash at the end of the epoch. Now bounded by the radius actually in use:
`hi = seen - WIN - max(2*WIN, ENC_POS_MAX)`. Verified at 2x/4x/8x/16x/32x
WIN, all OK.

Also committing probe_signature.py's workaround (it passes a reduced `seen`
so sampled positions stay in range without touching the sampler), since it
documents the failure independently.

CONTEXT -- why this knob now matters. The adaptive spawn threshold from the
previous commit cut domains 142 -> 53 and fragmentation 24x -> 11x, but did
NOT improve the partition: V-measure 0.42 -> 0.38, completeness 0.30 ->
0.28, silhouette -0.22 -> -0.25, still 0/38 genuine, and unlearn went from
LOCAL (+0.019 collateral) to LEAKED (+0.076). It merged harder, grouping
things that should not be grouped.

The decisive measurement is elsewhere: mean within-domain scatter 0.43
against mean between-domain distance 0.18 -- within-corpus spread is 2.4x
the between-corpus distance. No clustering algorithm recovers clusters that
overlap 2.4:1. The assembler thresholds were never the cause; the
signatures do not form corpus-level clusters, which is what the encoder's
64-256 byte positive radius would predict.

1e9c6b2280635e6d4bf3bbd669b8efad13a97b44
2026-07-25
fix: adaptive domain spawn threshold -- a fixed NEW_DIST made re-entry impossible
A full-warmup GH200 run on eng,py,num,c produced 142 live domains for 4
corpora, and the numbers say exactly why:

  mean within-domain cohesion 0.61  ->  within-domain distance 0.39
  NEW_DIST (spawn threshold)        =  0.35

A query re-entering its OWN domain sits 0.39 from that domain's centroid
while the spawn threshold is 0.35, so re-entry was arithmetically forced to
create a new domain every time. Every other symptom follows: boundary
detection is near-perfect (precision 0.98, recall 0.96) yet each boundary
CREATES instead of re-identifying; 24x fragmentation; silhouette -0.22 with
0/74 domains genuine, i.e. they are not distinct clusters at all; and
merging does fire (0.28 > the 0.17 separation) but creation outruns it, so
the population settles near 140.

Within-domain scatter is a property of the encoder and the data, and it
MOVES as the encoder trains, so the threshold cannot be a constant. It is
now measured: track the distances at which assignment actually happens and
spawn only on the high tail (median + DOM_SPAWN_K*MAD over a 512-sample
window), the same robust-deviation rule already used for self-consistency.
Falls back to NEW_DIST until 64 samples exist. DOM_ADAPTIVE=0 restores the
fixed threshold.

The same run also settled two other questions. The encoder DOES learn
separation when there is something to separate: 0.48 -> 0.75 across the
full 30k warmup on 4 domains, against a DECLINE (0.24 -> 0.18) on the
single-corpus run where there is nothing to separate -- so the earlier
"weak training" hypothesis was right and the full budget was needed to see
it. And the multi-domain throughput penalty is confirmed at 2.5x (3341
steps/min vs 7885-15598 single-domain), with contrastive_step at 69% of the
step, against a predicted ~2.7x.

_prof is cleared at every [rate] print, so its sum covers only the window

fix: completeness formula was homogeneity; guard TOKENIZER=1 without DATA_MODE=real
The metric I added last commit to stop over-segmentation hiding was itself
wrong. I computed H(true|domain) -- that is HOMOGENEITY, which behaves like
purity and is high for ANY pure-but-shattered clustering -- and printed it
as completeness. So a partition that was 16x fragmented reported

one true process in ONE domain. Verified on a synthetic 62-cluster /
4-class fragmentation: homogeneity 1.00, completeness 0.34, V 0.51 --
against 1.00 / 1.00 / 1.00 for the correct 4-cluster partition. All four
numbers are now printed.

AttributeError inside _retok, because TOK is only constructed on the
real-data branch. It now fails immediately naming the actual cause. This
bit a real launch -- a command missing DATA_MODE=real ran for a while and

5e02cfc113a56679462ca869a09ac76ca56ef07c
2026-07-25
fix: domain over-segmentation -- the assembler was partitioning by SPLICE SEGMENT, not by corpus
RETRACTION FIRST. I reported "domain assembly works, purity 0.54 -> 0.96"
and called the earlier failure an undertraining artifact. That was wrong.
Purity rises MONOTONICALLY with fragmentation -- one window per cluster
scores 1.0 -- so the number went up precisely BECAUSE the partition was
falling apart. Measured elsewhere: purity 1.00 at 1431 clusters with
completeness 0.18.

What was actually happening: build_stream emits 89-96 splice segments in a
120k stream, and the assembler produced 96 live domains. One domain per
SEGMENT. Mean domain size 9.5 windows, below MANAGE_MIN, so "per-domain
provenance" was a ~1.2 kB slice.

Four rules disagreed about what a domain is:
 1. SCALE -- _assign applied an absolute NEW_DIST=0.35 to a SINGLE window
    against centroids that are 40-window MEANS. Since contrastive_step's
    positive is only 64-256 bytes away (shorter than a 700-1800 byte
    segment), a well-trained encoder is taught to separate distant windows
    of the SAME corpus, so re-entry could not re-identify and always
    spawned. Encoder training makes this WORSE, which is the trend I
    misread as success.
 2. HYSTERESIS -- create at 0.35, merge at 0.12: every pair between was
    permanent. Merge now derives from MERGE_FRAC*NEW_DIST, one scale.
 3. IMMORTALITY -- `size` was cumulative and inflated on merge, so any
    domain reaching 15 windows was uncullable. Replaced with a DECAYED
    activity counter plus a grace period (ExpertRouter's existing rule).
    MANAGE_STALE 2000 -> 500; 2000 exceeded a 937-step pilot entirely, so
    zero culls could ever fire.
 4. NO CAP -- domains were the only population without a slot pool.
    MAX_DOMAINS=64; at cap, absorb into the nearest without dragging it.

Assign now queries the MEAN of the run that tripped the boundary rather
than its noisiest single window. _assign and manage are one matmul each
instead of O(N)/O(N^2) Python loops with a .item() sync per pair, and rekey
does one batched encode -- those loops doubled wall-clock at N~300, which a
long run reaches in minutes.

REPORTING: purity is now printed with COMPLETENESS, V-measure and an
explicit fragmentation ratio plus an OVER-SEGMENTED flag. Purity alone is
what let this hide for several turns.

ENC_POS_MAX added (default unchanged) to sweep the encoder's positive-pair
radius, which is the root cause. The trade is real and measured: at NP=4,
cross-domain positives run 9.8% at 2xWIN, 17.5% at 4x, 32.9% at 8x, 52.9%
at 16x. Wider buys corpus-scale invariance and costs contamination; the GPU
should settle it, not a guess.

2026-07-25
feat: KEY_LAYERS + cached causal mask; apply route_t on the chaining path
KEY_LAYERS unblocks the transformer, which is the only path with the
capacity for coherent English (L12 d768 = 85,054,464 non-embedding, exact
GPT-2-small parity, against the default GRU's 3.54M).

The transformer's LM step time already matched the GRU's (1.532 vs 1.572
ms at L4); it lost OVERALL only because _model_key ran the full stack on
thousands of KW=8 windows every step, in both the memory write and the
amortized rekey -- 99% of its measured deficit. KEY_LAYERS runs only the
first N blocks for the key path; the LM keeps every layer. Keys stay
mutually comparable because rekey re-encodes stored contexts through the
same truncated path. The causal mask is now cached instead of being
allocated and triu'd on every one of those calls.

Verified: MODEL=transformer LAYERS=6 KEY_LAYERS=2 trains, checkpoints and
evaluates (train 3.735 | held-out 3.804 | gap +0.069).

Separately, route_t was applied ONLY on the society path, so the chaining
path (SOCIETY=0) softmaxed raw logits at T=1.0. With N+1 near-equal logits
HALT starts at ~1/(N+1) and, being an ABSORBING operator, accumulates every
step -- which is most of the measured "halt 0.76, mean routed depth 0.24 of
4 steps". Now applied to the entry distribution and the transition matrix.

Also recorded: the hypothesis that InfoNCE positives straddling domain
boundaries explains the eng,py two-domain collapse is REFUTED. Measured
over 400k sampled pairs at SEG_MIN/MAX=700/1800, WIN=128: NP=2 gives 11.8%
straddling / 6.6% cross-domain, NP=4 gives 17.3% / 9.7%. Contamination is
HIGHER with four domains, so the effect runs opposite to what the
hypothesis requires.

c46a32f13840a1248bae6054e1466108c0bb34b6
2026-07-25
feat: fetch_40g.sh + resumable fetch_big.py for the multi-epoch corpus
A 40 GB streamed pull is a multi-hour job, and fetch_big.py was not
resumable: it always opened part000 and re-streamed from document 0, so a
failure at 30 GB meant starting over and silently overwriting what was
already there.

fetch_big.py now records (bytes, shard, docs) to a manifest after every
shard and, with --resume, skips the consumed documents via
IterableDataset.skip() and continues at the next shard index. Skipping
still walks the stream but neither decodes nor writes, so it is far
cheaper than re-downloading. Unit-tested against a stubbed datasets
module: RESUME banner fires, ds.skip(4321) is called, writing starts at
part003, part000 is not clobbered, and the manifest advances.

fetch_40g.sh wraps it for an unattended run: checks free disk against
target+20%+5GB before starting, creates a THROWAWAY venv for `datasets`
(it pulls numpy/pyarrow/pandas, which on aarch64 can upgrade numpy under
the torch you train with and ABI-clash, for a package the training path
never imports), runs detached under nohup so it survives logout, and
offers status/stop subcommands. Re-running continues rather than restarts.

Also: the "next command" fetch_big.py prints suggested D_MODEL_B, which
self_organize.py only reads as an alias -- it now emits D_MODEL, the name
that was silently ignored and ran every early benchmark at d=128.

0b08b74e0e3d44baaabaafd8ce9da508c99f4243
2026-07-25
fix: GH200 readiness -- aarch64 torch trap, the memory blend rule, and the generation path
THE GH200 TRAP. PyPI ships one linux-aarch64 torch wheel per release and up
to 2.10.0 it is CPU-ONLY: every CUDA dependency is gated
platform_machine=="x86_64", the wheel is 97-139 MiB against 730-860 for
x86, and it imports cleanly while torch.cuda.is_available() returns False.
`torch>=2.1` was satisfied by exactly such a wheel, so a GH200 run would
have trained on the Grace CPU and merely looked slow. Pinned torch>=2.11
(the cutover is exact); bench_gpu.sh now gates on CUDA before the 1 GB
fetch instead of failing five arms one at a time; numpy dropped (nothing on
the product path imports it, and >=1.21 resolves to 2.x with ABI-clash
risk against torch).

THE MEMORY BLEND WAS UNCONDITIONAL. read() scatters a softmax, so dist
always sums to exactly 1.0 -- verified numerically. hp = dist.sum() was
therefore identically 1.0 and the blend was a fixed 50/50 mix at every
position however bad the nearest neighbour, while `conf` (the real
similarity) was computed and discarded by all three call sites. Now gated
on conf. Measured effect is small (-0.168 -> -0.146) because at this store
size retrieval genuinely returns poor neighbours with high cosine anyway,
so this is a partial fix, not a solved problem.

THE GENERATION PATH, third and fourth defects. prompt.py routed the fabric
through the NON-grounded q_entry path while training routes by centroid
cosine -- different functions, and in grounded mode the parameters
prompt.py used receive no gradient at all. It also blended hidden states
where training ensembles at the OUTPUT (each expert's own head). Both now
match, fab_cfg records the routing mode so a checkpoint cannot be loaded
under the wrong one, and society() is sparse there too.

Blocking items for LAYERS=12: final LayerNorm in TransformerEncoder
(mirrored in prompt.py, or checkpoints load into a different network);
@torch.no_grad() on selfcheck, which was building a 12-layer autograd graph
over every stored entry; FAB_HID_MULT float-parsed with an int() at the
point of use so 0.5 no longer crashes.

preflight.sh hardened: asserts sm_90 in get_arch_list, checks driver r580+
for CUDA 13, and executes the actual cuDNN GRU(B=1,T=256,d=768) fwd/bwd --
the kernel that was 46% of the A100 step -- plus a synthetic mmap probe at
the host's real page size that needs no data present.

Verified: transformer + 8-node fabric + grounded routing trains,
checkpoints and generates end to end.

ff8754a4ab74db1af938bc0a0b28f067b58782bb
2026-07-25
feat: preflight.sh -- fail loudly on a fresh box before burning GPU hours
Every check corresponds to something that has actually gone wrong here: a
benchmark that silently ran at d=128 because D_MODEL_B was read by nothing;
a bench arm that died on a missing /usr/bin/time; a multi-day run that
would have seen 2 MB because CORPUS_CAP defaults to 2000000.

The GH200 adds a new class of risk: this repo has only ever run on x86, and
Grace is aarch64 with 64KB pages. Preflight reports arch, page size, core
count and host RAM, verifies a real CUDA matmul rather than just
torch.cuda.is_available(), re-runs the datastream mmap probe under the
host's actual page size, and checks the shell tools bench_gpu.sh depends on.

The last check is the one worth having: it greps self_organize.py for every
knob a launch command sets and fails if any of them is read by NOTHING.
That is exactly the D_MODEL_B failure mode, and it is the single most
expensive class of bug this project has hit. Currently 56/56 are read.

ef412e2e4f1127f120793c0ea3636451b214dd19
2026-07-24
fix: persist and correctly restore the per-expert memory partition
own/last/tick/n_own/quota are now saved, and the restore rebuilds the
partition IN PLACE at owner*quota+slot rather than going through the
compacting path. That mattered: the existing restore packs active entries
into the first N slots, which under a partitioned store would have
reassigned every entry to the wrong owner block and silently destroyed the
per-expert structure on resume. Restore is refused outright if the
checkpoint's n_own does not match the live store.

Latent until MEM_PER_EXPERT is switched on, but it would have been a silent
corruption exactly at the point the feature became useful.

242e021716e1bc79516490ed13dc20d81a74fe8f
2026-07-24
feat: per-expert LRU memory (implemented, default OFF on measurement)
Implements the requested design: the store partitions into n_own blocks of
MEM_QUOTA entries, an entry lives at owner*quota + slot, the owner is the
argmax-routed expert, and eviction is per-owner LRU on LAST-USE TIME. That
last point matters -- self.use is a decayed retrieval COUNT (an LFU signal,
decayed by write count rather than time), so a real LRU needed a new `last`
tick stamped on every read. Free slots fill first; a window presenting more
survivors than the quota keeps the most surprising rather than letting its
own tail evict rows written microseconds earlier.

Writes are compartmentalized per expert; READS stay global. That is the
"partially, not fully, isolate" property -- an expert's knowledge is its own
to keep and to lose, but any query can still reach it.

Default is OFF, on measurement. Same seed, same config, only the store
differs:

  global 200k slots  ->  memory contributes -0.097 b/B
  32 owners x 64     ->  memory contributes -0.652 b/B

The partition costs 0.555 b/B at the scale tested, so it does not become the
default path until it earns it. A separate pre-existing finding: memory is
already slightly net-negative even with the global store, which is worth
knowing independently of this change.

A follow-up run (global capped to 2048 vs partitioned 2048) is in flight to
separate the capacity effect from the partitioning effect, i.e. whether the
fix is a larger quota or a different ownership rule.

Co-Authored-By: Claude <noreply@anthropic.com>
Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01PksNp6ECrYm34zjV2rbm4x

020c1572f6d9a130d13766492725b127cc71a1ff
2026-07-24
feat: burst growth to a large expert population, and three fixes that make it mean something
Growth. PlateauGrowth is replaced by the state machine requested: WATCH
detects an UNEXPECTED worsening (loss above the slow EMA by z robust
deviations, running MAD, so it is scale-free and does not fire on gradient
noise); BURST grows FAB_BURST=3 at once rather than one; RECOVER refuses to
re-arm while the model is re-learning, since the burst's own transient
worsening would otherwise re-trigger it immediately, and exits only on a
stall. An early RAMP guarantees rapid initial growth, and note_shift()
marks retokenisation and epoch resampling so self-inflicted loss jumps are
not mistaken for distribution shifts. FAB_WARMUP 2000->300, FAB_COOLDOWN
1500->400, FAB_NMAX 8->64 (the old cap existed because dense execution made
a large population unaffordable; sparse top-k removed that reason).
Verified: 3 -> 57 experts.

Three fixes without which those experts are decorative:

- Fabric.remove(j) rebuilt bodies/keys/qproj but never pruned cent, so
  society() read cent[:N] against the shifted body list and every expert
  above j was routed by its neighbour's region. Silent misrouting of the
  whole population after any removal, and a corrupted independence test.

- Routing was near-uniform at ANY N. Signature and centroid are unit
  vectors in SIG_D=64, so cosine logits have std ~1/sqrt(64)=0.125 and at
  ROUTE_T=1.0 the top-vs-mean weight ratio is ~1.37x regardless of N -- at
  N=64 that is w ~= 0.016 +/- 12%, so top-k selects noise and nothing can
  specialize. Default lowered to 0.1. This is a better explanation of the
  historical "0 exclusive experts" and "12/17 idle" than any of the growth
  theories.

- A burst seeds several experts at one signature, so they were born as
  exact clones with identical regions and could never differentiate.
  BIRTH_JITTER=0.15.

Growth events are now labelled by cause (ramp / REGRESSION / stall) rather
than all reported as regressions.

9b05bd315fec162c335570d3e72fb6088e70de54
2026-07-24
perf+fix: sparse top-k experts, and revive the routing parameters that received no gradient
Two findings from re-reading the fabric, both measured.

1. The society computed every expert and used two. The caller formed its
   logits from _O[:, j] for the top ENS_K=2 experts and assigned the dense
   blend to h, where nothing read it -- so every expert beyond the k-th was
   computed, discarded and never gradiented. That is why raising the expert
   count changed nothing. society() now computes only the top-k routed
   experts and returns (w, O_k, idx); cost matches the selection that was
   already happening, which is what makes a large population affordable.
   The ENS_K and IND_K head passes are also cached rather than duplicated.

2. Routing received no gradient at all. With ROUTE_GROUNDED=1 (the default)
   the router ran off the centroid buffer, updated by EMA under no_grad,
   against a signature that sig_of already detaches. Measured: keys, qproj,
   q_entry, nov, ctrl and halt_key all reported "no grad". The documented
   learned transition matrix, absorbing HALT and recurrent routing query
   were inert in the configuration that runs.

   route_w() now adds a per-expert bilinear term qproj[i](gist).keys[i] to
   the centroid cosine, reviving those parameters. Grounding is kept and
   gist stays detached, so the gradient reaches the router's parameters but
   never back into the SigEncoder. Verified: keys.* and qproj.* now carry
   gradient. ROUTE_LEARN=0 restores the frozen clustering router.

Verified end-to-end at FAB_N0=6: checkpoint saved, memorization check
intact, node mass spread across all six.
