dd7ceb0c605838facf3c54e6a000ebbaddf6c707
2026-07-24
fix: memory provenance positions were byte/token misaligned, so grounded lookups read the wrong text
mem.pos was written as arange(bpos, bpos + WIN). bpos is a BYTE coordinate
(tok_bs[i]) but the arange steps once per TOKEN. Under the online
tokenizer a token averages ~1.85 bytes, so by the end of a WIN=256 window
the recorded provenance had drifted 200+ bytes, while prompt.py's _recall
reads only a 220-byte span around the stored position. Grounded passage
retrieval was therefore pointing at roughly the wrong text, and the same
positions are what make provenance-based unlearning meaningful.

_bp now carries (byte_pos, token_index) and _posv() builds the true
per-token byte positions from tok_bs, falling back to the old arange when
not in online-tokenizer mode (where the stream is bytes and the two
coincide). Verified end-to-end: checkpoint saved, memorization check
intact.

Found while grounding the self-training design against the code, not by
testing -- the same class of defect as the five in the previous commit.

535f5f6a3aebe0ed834267ece045da2d46955726
2026-07-24
fix: five critical defects that made the multi-epoch run test something other than the system
A full capability audit answered "does the multi-epoch test contain
everything?" with no. Five critical defects, two of which invalidated the
coherence evidence.

1. Generation ran on a mutilated model. The expert-independence test calls
   fab.remove() on the BUSIEST expert and never restored it, so every eval
   after that point -- including the generation samples used to judge
   coherence -- ran on the damaged model. Now deep-copied, ablated,
   measured and restored. (load_state_dict cannot repopulate a ModuleList
   that remove() shrank, so the restore swaps the containers directly.)

2. fab.cent was a plain attribute, not a buffer. With ROUTE_GROUNDED=1 --
   the default -- those centroids ARE the routing function, and they were
   absent from state_dict(), so never saved, never resumed, never moved to
   device. prompt.py routed every generation with untrained centroids.

3. EXPERTS=1 is a no-op whenever FABRIC=1: the forward pass is an elif
   chain, so the bank never receives gradient while the end-of-run report
   still prints expert counts. Now warned at startup.

4. CORPUS_CAP defaults to 2MB and the product script never sets it, so a
   multi-day run would train on 2MB of text. Warned.

5. EPOCHS>1 only resamples under DISK_STREAM=1; otherwise every epoch is a
   byte-identical replay. Warned.

Also: _save_ckpt is now atomic (tmp + os.replace) keeping one previous
generation, since a kill mid-save destroyed the only checkpoint and the
tokenizer that decodes it; and write() now routes its gating through
_gate() so WRITE_QUANTILE applies on both write paths rather than only the
batched one.

All five warnings verified firing, and the restore verified end-to-end.

3b7844ded295e60d8af14145b4de60c1d3bff489
2026-07-24
fix: single-domain runs crashed in the eval battery; add live domain/boundary counter
The English-only configuration -- the one chosen for the proof-of-concept
run -- died after training with:

  IndexError: Cannot choose from an empty sequence
  self_organize.py:1317  random.choice([z for z in procs if z != p])

The wrongness-injection test builds a synthetic wrong pair from a context
in one process and a continuation in a different one, which is undefined
with a single source. It threw after training and the checkpoint had
completed, taking the rest of the eval battery (generation, unlearn,
verification) with it. It now detects fewer than two processes, skips the
synthetic injection with an explicit message, and continues; self
consistency still runs on the genuine store.

Also added domains/boundaries to the [rate] line. On a single-domain
corpus the byte-level signature may never shift enough to trigger a
boundary, which would leave domain assembly, provenance and per-domain
unlearning untested for the whole run. A small-scale English-only run
assembled 1 live domain with 0 boundaries detected across 161 splices,
which is not a verdict at that size but is a real risk worth watching.
Surfacing the counts live turns a multi-day unknown into an hour-one
signal.

c1348302cc497f4a99e0fb46cbc1c59da734a9ad
2026-07-24
fix: quantile write gate (WRITE_TARGET was silently ignored) + tail-only retokenization
Two fixes for the English proof-of-concept run.

1. WRITE_TARGET was not honored. Surprise is 1 - p_model(true token), so
   with V=16384 an undertrained model sits near 1.0 almost everywhere; the
   additive controller drives gate_theta straight into gate_ceil=0.95 and
   the kept fraction runs 1.00/0.93/0.80 instead of the requested 0.12,
   filling MEM_CAP by step ~831 rather than ~6510. An absolute threshold
   cannot track a distribution squeezed against 1.0. The gate now tracks
   the (1 - target) QUANTILE of the surprise distribution, which is
   scale-free and hits the target by construction, held as an EMA so a dull
   stretch still writes less and a surprising one more -- the "relative
   surprise" behaviour the additive controller was reaching for. Kept on
   device, so it adds no per-window host sync. WRITE_QUANTILE=0 reverts.

   This also cuts the rekey cost, which scales with the number of active
   entries.

2. Retokenization re-segmented the whole byte stream every RETOK_EVERY
   steps, so its cost scaled with STREAM_LEN -- roughly a x0.77 throughput
   tax at a 10MB stream and x0.25 at 100MB -- for work that is pure waste,
   since the consumed prefix is never read again in that epoch. It now
   re-segments only the unconsumed tail. Safe because DynamicTokenizer
   minting is append-only: existing ids keep their meaning, so a stream
   whose prefix used the older vocab still decodes correctly, which is what
   source.bin needs. The index i is unchanged because the prefix is
   preserved verbatim. RETOK_TAIL=0 reverts.

Together these remove the RETOK_EVERY-vs-STREAM_LEN tension, so a long run
can use a large stream without a growing retokenization tax.

A/B in progress; no numbers claimed here.

9a7e1c81dd87df80a4045aa0ff5acff40c3110d1
2026-07-24
perf: SIG_BATCH -- batch sig_of over the span where the encoder is provably frozen
The corrected d=768 bench put sig_of at 46% of the loop: a batch-1
SigEncoder GRU over WIN=256, once per step, costing ~4.7 ms/step and
invariant to model type, AMP, ENC_FUSE and REKEY_CHUNK. It is the single
largest cost, and the one the earlier d=128 bench hid.

The batching interval is deliberately NOT BATCH_W. enc.parameters() are
written only by contrastive_step (asm.rekey reads it, never writes), so
the span from one firing to the next is an interval over which the encoder
is provably constant, and every window in it is encoded under exactly the
parameters the sequential loop would have used. _sig_horizon computes that
span from the shift-gate cadence, and a detected boundary clears the queue
-- which closes the sig -> boundary -> cadence -> sig feedback loop rather
than ignoring it. The queue is also cleared on re-tokenization and on the
per-epoch resample, since both replace the stream the lookahead indexed.

Expected ~1.55-1.71x on config A. Equivalence is expected to be
mathematically-equivalent rather than bit-identical, for the same reason as
ENC_FUSE and KEY_BATCH: batching changes the GRU's GEMM shape. The A/B in
progress runs the 4-domain mix with RETOK_EVERY=200 and two epochs, which
exercises boundary invalidation, re-tokenization and epoch wrap together.
No numbers are claimed here until it reports.

test: KEY_BATCH A/B -- equivalence measured, and no CPU speedup, as predicted

  mem_tok / mem_src / mem_pos / mem_ctx : bit-identical
  model weights                          : bit-identical
  mem_keys                               : 82 of 23237 rows differ
                                           max abs 4.2e-8
                                           min per-key cosine 0.99999976

So "exactly faithful" was again slightly too strong: batching changes the

is ~1000x tighter than ENC_FUSE's and, unlike ENC_FUSE, non-compounding --
stored keys are detached, nothing trains on them, which is why the model
weights came out identical rather than diverging.

this is a dispatch-count fix and CPU has no comparable dispatch overhead.

re-run at the corrected d=768.

a46c045da9870155cf26bb24fd405c1b4e6f6a26
2026-07-24
perf: batch the memory-key encodes -- attack call count, which is what the A100 profile blamed
The A100 profile attributed 48-72% of the loop to memory key + rekey, and
the mechanism was call COUNT rather than FLOPs: _model_key ran ~1952 times
per 976 steps on tiny tensors against ~61 real LM forwards.

mem.write_batch(): one key encode per batch instead of BATCH_W of them.
The gate now runs for every window first, in the same order, so gate_theta
evolves identically; the survivors are concatenated; a single _model_key
call encodes all of them; the stores then proceed per window as before.
Exactly faithful -- the encoder is row-independent, so a row's key does not
depend on which other rows share its batch. write() and write_batch() share
a single _store() so the two cannot drift. KEY_BATCH=0 restores per-window.

REKEY_CHUNK: do C steps' worth of re-keying in one call every C steps.
Identical total work and identical per-entry refresh rate, but an entry's
refresh can land up to C steps later than before, so this is a timing
change rather than an equivalence and defaults to 1 (the old cadence).
At the default the code path is arithmetically identical to what it
replaced.

Together these take the per-LM-step call count from ~32 toward ~2.

Note this is a dispatch-count fix and CPU does not have the dispatch
overhead that made it expensive on the GPU, so the CPU A/B in progress
tests equivalence, not speed. The speed number has to come from the GPU at
the corrected d=768.

bench: report param count, fix the LAYERS confound, flag that the 85% figure is data-dependent
Three corrections to the bench before it is run for real.

LAYERS defaults to 4 for transformer and 1 for GRU, so comparing config A
against C would have compared models differing ~8x in parameter count and
produced a confounded headline. LAYERS is now set explicitly per config
and shown in the header, the [BENCH] line reports parameter count so speed
and size can be read apart, and TRF_LAYERS=1 reruns C/D at matched depth.

More importantly, the ~85% encoder share does not generalize.
contrastive_step is shift-gated: it runs every step near a detected domain
boundary and every ENC_EVERY_IDLE (12) steps when the stream is stable.
Every profile so far used the 4-domain eng/py/num/c mix, which switches
constantly, so the encoder was effectively always at the dense cadence. A
short single-domain run drops it to 33% (encoder 33 | memkey 22 | lm 20 |
sig_of 18) as the gate throttles it.

So the bottleneck ranking depends on which data mix the run uses, and the
bench -- which uses single-domain fineweb-edu -- may legitimately not
reproduce it. The summary sheet now says so, rather than letting the
number be carried over blindly.

feat: GPU throughput bench (bench_gpu.sh) + BENCH=1 early exit
Prep for a GPU session whose purpose is to find where the time goes,
before renting the machine for the multi-epoch run.

bench_gpu.sh runs five short configs on a fresh box -- fetching 1GB of
text if none is present -- and summarizes:

  A  gru          fp32  fuse=1   baseline, current defaults
  B  gru          bf16  fuse=1   does bf16 help the GRU path
  C  transformer  fp32  fuse=1   does parallel-over-sequence beat the GRU
  D  transformer  bf16  fuse=1   both together
  E  gru          fp32  fuse=0   what the fused encoder pass is worth here

It samples nvidia-smi during each run. GPU utilization well below ~40%
alongside a slow step means the card is idling between kernel launches,
which would confirm the step is launch-bound and that a bigger GPU is not
the fix. That claim has so far only been made from arithmetic.

BENCH=1 stops right after the training loop and reports steps/min, kB/s,
GB/day, peak GPU memory and the per-component profile. The eval battery
(final re-tokenization, memorization check, generation, unlearn tests) is
a large fixed cost that would otherwise swamp a short timing run.

Defaults match the pilot's real shape (D_MODEL_B=768 WIN=256 BATCH_W=16),
since component ranking shifts with model size and measuring at toy scale
would mislead.

c95e187b884d04bfd72fd011d6138c6a81c9e1df
2026-07-24
perf: isolate the two contrastive_step changes -- gather is bit-identical, fuse is not
Running gather-only (ENC_FUSE=0) against the pre-change code, seeded:
encoder weights, model weights and memory all bit-identical. So the two
halves carry different guarantees, and nearly all the speed sits in the
half that costs bit-identity:

  ENC_FUSE=0  ->  69s vs 70s baseline (~1%),  BIT-IDENTICAL
  ENC_FUSE=1  ->  62s/64s vs 70s   (~11%),  mathematically equivalent

The device-resident gather is therefore free and strictly safe, and the
~1e-5 encoder drift reported in the previous commit is attributable to
the fused pass alone. Default stays ENC_FUSE=1; set 0 for the strict
guarantee.

On GPU the fuse should pay more than it does here: it halves the number
of sequential GRU launches, and launch latency is what dominates a GPU
step, whereas on CPU it only improves BLAS and threading efficiency.

62e78d914477760af3caf02d460892f2efb9df70
2026-07-24
perf: add ENC_FUSE flag; the encoder fusion is equivalent but not bit-identical
Measured effect of the contrastive_step changes, same config and seed:
wall 70s/73s -> 62s/64s (~12%), encoder share 87%/86% -> 81%/82%.

The equivalence check came back weaker than the one for KEY_PREGATE, and
the earlier "exactly equivalent" wording was too strong. Old code vs new,
seeded: memory (keys/tok/src/pos/ctx over 23707 entries) and model weights
are identical, but the encoder's own weights differ by ~1e-5 relative.

The cause is that fusing the anchor and positive batches into one pass
changes the GRU kernel's batch shape and therefore its reduction order.
Float addition is not associative, so the result is mathematically
equivalent but not bit-for-bit. Nothing discrete diverged at this scale --
same domains, same memory contents, same LM weights -- but rounding can
compound over a long run, so ENC_FUSE=0 restores the two-pass form and
with it the bit-level guarantee.

The gather-from-resident-tensor half is expected to be bit-identical on
its own; that isolation is still running and is not claimed here.

c4f525c6e99896eaf1f9fa1ad34d5e942389a7df
2026-07-24
perf: cut the real bottleneck -- contrastive_step, measured at 87% of the loop
The profiler contradicted the previous commit's premise. Measured share of
loop wall-clock (CPU, D=256 WIN=128 BATCH_W=8 KEY_WIN=32):

  encoder(contrastive) 87% | memory key+write 4% | lm fwd+bwd 4%
  sig_of 4% | rekey(amortized) 1%

ENC_EVERY defaults to 1, so the SigEncoder's InfoNCE step runs every step
over 2*ENC_BATCH windows -- roughly 12x the language model's own forward
work, plus a backward pass. The language model is 4% of its own training
loop. The memory-key change in the previous commit is correct and proven
equivalent, but it optimized a 4% component and moved wall-clock 70s->73s,
i.e. not at all.

Two changes to contrastive_step, both exactly equivalent:

- enc(A) and enc(P) were two separate encoder passes; they are now one
  concatenated pass. The encoder is row-independent, so the result is
  identical, at half the sequential GRU launches.
- Both batches were built out of Python lists -- 2*ENC_BATCH*WIN int
  conversions per step, then copied to the device. They are now gathered
  from a device-resident copy of the sequence (set_enc_tensor, refreshed
  on every resample), which produces bit-identical batches.

Measurement and the seeded equivalence proof are still outstanding; no
speedup is claimed here until both have been run.

Co-Authored-By: Claude <noreply@anthropic.com>

---
457c9d0b312dec38ac2c3c064e9d0218d5e1dfe5
2026-07-24
perf: profile the loop, encode memory keys behind the surprise gate, drop per-step host syncs
Instrumentation first (PROFILE=1): per-component wall-clock attribution
printed alongside the [rate] line, so tuning targets what is measurably
slow rather than what looks slow.

mem_key(x) encoded a key for every position -- (BATCH_W*WIN, KW) through
the LM -- and mem.write then discarded the ~88% that fail the surprise
gate. write() now accepts key_fn and encodes after the gate, so only the
survivors pay. This is exactly equivalent: the encoder is row-independent,
so a row's key does not depend on which other rows share its batch, and
the gate, its controller and the resulting entries are untouched.
Verified by seeded A/B (KEY_PREGATE=0 vs 1): mem_keys, mem_tok, mem_src,
mem_pos, mem_ctx and all model weights bit-identical over 20364 entries.

Removed per-step GPU->CPU synchronizations, each of which drains the async
CUDA queue: dom_exp accumulates on device and moves to host once in the
end-of-run report; _fab_nov stays a 0-dim device tensor; the independence
weight uses .detach() rather than float() (identical numerics, both stop
the gradient); the loss scalar is fetched once per step instead of twice.

Added TF32 (default on; matmul TF32 is off by default in current torch)
and AMP=bf16 as an opt-in autocast for the LM step. Memory keys stay fp32
deliberately -- retrieval is a dot product over normalized keys, the one
place reduced precision would change behaviour rather than just speed.

Co-Authored-By: Claude <noreply@anthropic.com>
Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01PksNp6ECrYm34zjV2rbm4x

---
59c6cf41aa15b836df05b156b0f56031e2c0a3bc
2026-07-24
fix: make the checkpoint complete (world model + RESUME) and the ETA honest
Audit of the pilot's own code path found three gaps before launching a
multi-day run:

1. The world model was never checkpointed. With WORLD_FEEDBACK=1 the base
   LM is trained as `h += world_proj(forecast)`, but the saved checkpoint
   had no world state and prompt.py had no world path -- so generation ran
   a different network than training, silently invalidating any coherence
   judgement. Now saved (including the grown population size) and applied
   in prompt.py at the same point as training, before fabric/head.

2. Checkpoints were generate-only: there was no way to continue training
   from one, so a run that died late lost everything. RESUME=runs/x now
   restores model/enc/fabric/experts/world, both optimizer states, step,
   the memory store and the domain centroids, re-growing fabric nodes and
   dynamics predictors to their saved sizes before the optimizers are
   built so Adam moments land in the right param groups.

3. Resume would have corrupted the vocabulary: under TOK_ONLINE=1 the
   tokenizer always re-seeded from scratch, so a restored embedding table
   would have been indexed by a different vocabulary. RESUME now loads the
   saved dyntok.json.

Verified end-to-end on CPU: train -> checkpoint -> resume, exit 0, Adam
step counters continued 937->1171 rather than resetting to 1.

Also: [probe] extrapolated from a synthetic batch-1 LM forward/backward
and ignored sig_of, the live contrastive encoder, the amortized re-key,
domain assembly and memory -- which is why its estimates were always
optimistic. It is now labelled a lower bound, and a [rate] meter measures
the real loop (steps/min, kB/s, self-correcting ETA, GB of text per day).

Co-Authored-By: Claude <noreply@anthropic.com>
Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01PksNp6ECrYm34zjV2rbm4x

eae40cae9569ea24668669ce1fc531fd88c3e511
2026-07-24
feat: disk-streaming data loader (DISK_STREAM) -- data disk-bounded, not RAM-bounded
Removes the RAM ceiling on training data (the blocker for GPT-2-scale
data). datastream.py: MmapConcat presents on-disk corpus files as one
indexable byte sequence via mmap (disk-paged, not resident) -- CPU-probe
verified byte-identical to reading everything into RAM.

Integrated (DISK_STREAM=1, gated off):
- Corpus is mmap-backed: CORPUS_CAP can exceed RAM; only the current
  STREAM_LEN slice is resident.
- Each epoch RE-SAMPLES a fresh STREAM_LEN slice from the big corpus, so
  EPOCHS x STREAM_LEN unique tokens are covered without holding it all in
  RAM. Refactored _retok/_resample for per-epoch stream rebuild.
- SEG_LEN bounds sampling to the train head without slicing the mmap into
  RAM; VAL_CAP caps the materialized held-out set; memorization-check
  train sample fixed to stay in the train region under mmap.
- In-RAM path unchanged when DISK_STREAM=0 (default).

CPU-smoke-verified end-to-end: epoch 2 drew a fresh sample, memorization
gap +0.110 (correct split), domains on, exit 0.

a1767b727b7a25526465cd3dbf6c8741dd3b93b7
2026-07-24
fix: world-model collapse bug + RECON_W=0 waste + clean EPOCHS mechanism
Fills the known gaps, each CPU-verified:
- World-model collapse: the integration scaled the anti-collapse
  (variance/decorrelation) term by WORLD_W=0.1, running it at 1/10
  strength -> latent collapsed (std 0.24). Now applied at full strength
  via WORLD_VAR (default 1.0). Verified: std 0.24->0.97, forward-pred vs
  persistence +13.6%->+34.1%.
- RECON_W=0 waste: guard the per-step Reconstructor term with RECON_W>0,
  skipping a redundant key-encode multiplied by 0 on the default path.
- Multi-epoch gap: the loop did a single pass. Added EPOCHS=N (build the
  stream once, reset to start N times, step keeps counting) -- more
  memory-efficient than STREAM_LEN=N*corpus resampling. Verified:
  EPOCHS=2 does two clean passes.

Still open: async CUDA-stream rekey overlap (GPU-only, can't verify in
the CPU sandbox).

98e3a549c98dac97eb9b554bcd3210b3cd282aff
2026-07-24
perf: no-compromise fixes -- amortized rekey + shift-gated encoder (keep full functionality)
Corrects the earlier compromise-y approach. These keep ALL functionality
(full learned encoder, full domains, full drift-survival):

- Amortized rekey (REKEY_AMORTIZED=1, default): spread the same
  whole-store re-encode across steps via a rotating cursor instead of a
  spike every REKEY_EVERY. Same per-entry refresh rate, same freshness,
  no periodic stall. Also skips entries that can't be read
  (is_wrong/is_unverified) -- pure-waste elimination, no retrieval loss.
- Shift-gated encoder (ENC_EVERY_IDLE/ENC_SHIFT_WIN): throttle the
  SigEncoder when the stream is stable, snap back to dense updates on a
  detected boundary. Full responsiveness, less redundant work.

Domains stay ON: SELF_ORG defaults to 1; the disable switch is now only
an optional experiment (user withdrew the disable). CPU-smoke-verified
with domains on (38 live domains, no crash). Async CUDA-stream overlap
not done -- GPU-only, can't verify in the CPU sandbox.

Two non-compromising cost fixes, both CPU-smoke-verified:
- Adaptive warmup: stop the (30k) SigEncoder warmup once the separation

  prediction, so a language-capability run can turn them off. Smoke: 0
  live domains, run + unlearn clean.

(only provenance/editing); the memory write-gate stores ~40% of all
positions (WRITE_TARGET=0.4), which is why the store is huge and rekey is
costly -- lower it to make memory selective.

9df85b831bfa36b40843fa3a8c4e68a9d4f84682
2026-07-21
fix: Verification failed in the product loop; fit Reconstructor post-hoc
Full product-loop GPU test revealed Verification FAILED there (0.3% precision,
worse than B's 1%) despite the standalone's 100% - and VERIFY_SWEEP gutted the
store (~21k/292k deleted, mostly genuine).

Root cause: the Reconstructor was trained JOINTLY during the loop, but the
online tokenizer re-tokenizes the stream (256->6176) and keys get re-keyed, so
the genuine-association manifold is a moving target -> the signal is noise.

Fix: verify() now FITS the Reconstructor POST-HOC on the final, settled store
(VERIFY_FIT=3000), reproducing the standalone's winning condition; joint
training off by default (RECON_W=0). CPU-smoke-tested. Needs a GPU re-test with
VERIFY_SWEEP=0 until precision is re-confirmed.

The standalone result (AUC 0.980) stands - only the integration was broken.
Recorded honestly in STATE section 7 + changelog.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 <noreply@anthropic.com>
Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01PksNp6ECrYm34zjV2rbm4x

---
5cad71a627c56a65b583993e2c887912ef5ac9da
2026-07-21
probe: keystone validated - functional embedding via cross-content transfer
keystone_probe.py: tests whether an embedding can organize by FUNCTION (the
operation) rather than CONTENT (the operands) - the make-or-break for
routing/reuse.

Result (CPU synthetic): an embedding trained as a REUSABLE code that must
transfer across content (derive z from one input->output pair, require it to
transform a NEW input under the same op) hits k-NN op-purity 0.80 vs 0.50
surface (chance 0.20, gap +0.30). Naive same-input coding gave only 0.61 (z
cheated with content) - cross-content TRANSFER is the key, and concretely
realizes the design's 'modification before embedding' step.

So functional similarity is learnable; routing/reuse is buildable. Recorded in
STATE section 7 + changelog; updated the routing / what-is-missing direction
files. Toy synthetic - real router integration is future work.

213820deb93c11be9f70fa162aeac8b84126f4ef
2026-07-21
add self-contained console A/B test; reconstruction validated on real data (CPU)
verify_console_test.py: self-contained (torch + data/ only, no repo imports,
no git pull; paste via exec(open(...).read())). Trains a small GRU on the real
corpora, then compares reconstruction vs self-consistency B at flagging
injected corruption.

Building it caught a methodology error: the first version used 50% cross-domain
corruption = the EASY regime B already handles (~97%). Rewrote it faithful:
surprise-gated genuine negatives (B's actual failure mode) + base-rate-honest
metrics (AUC, precision at 1% base). CPU result on real data (undertrained toy
GRU): reconstruction AUC 0.978 vs B 0.903; precision@1% 100% vs 30.5% -- the
reframe holds (reconstruction is decoupled from surprise and does not
false-positive on surprise-gated genuine entries). GPU run remains the real
validation. Recorded in STATE section 7 + changelog.

fbdcd50123bc1925168d4907559f70f394a56d00
2026-07-21
build: Verification (reconstruction) - Reconstructor + opt-in wiring, CPU-validated
First code change. Builds Verification (renamed from B) as reconstruction-based
verification, decoupled from surprise.

- verification.py: Reconstructor (reverse embedder) that CROSS-reconstructs the
  expected token-code from the context key; reconstruction error = the verify
  signal. recon_loss + verify(). Standalone CPU probe: separates genuine vs
  corrupt associations at AUC ~0.93 on structured data (the naive joint
  autoencoder gave only ~0.65 - the key dominated and diluted the token signal;
  caught on CPU and fixed to cross-reconstruction before any GPU run).
- memory.py: recon field + set_recon + is_unverified (adaptive threshold,
  parallels is_wrong); reset on write.
- self_organize.py: VERIFY flag (default 'selfcon' = old behavior unchanged;
  'recon' opts in), instantiate Reconstructor, add to optimizer, train it in the
  LM loop, and report reconstruction precision vs the old self-consistency B in
  the wrongness test. Additive + opt-in: default runs are byte-identical.
- End-to-end CPU smoke (VERIFY=recon, synthetic) runs; 14.6% precision on a tiny
  undertrained model vs old B ~1%. Real validation is the GPU A/B.

Deferred behind the first green GPU test (per 'as wide as comfortable before
testing'): retire_stale wiring, release-don't-kill deletion, per-domain quota.

Three more design directions from the user:

  domains, experts, tools) - the thread toward a much smaller architecture

628dfc5c69ecc0a5088a17e3067a2b6548cb10ea
2026-07-21
handoff: refine directions - subcontracting via router; senses at tokenizer layer
Two refinements to the design directions:
- The full task should not be solved alone by any expert; it is
  subcontracted and spread across the router base (division of labor at the
  sub-task level). This revises the independence-loss premise: safety
  against incorrect removal comes from shared, reusable sub-skills rather
  than whole-task generalists. Flagged on the GLOSSARY independence-loss
  entry so it isn't treated as fixed.
- Senses live at the lowest tokenizer layer, discovered on unknown/unusual
  input (surprise-triggered), before a reconcile -> understand pipeline.

Updated both design-directions files, the GLOSSARY, and STATE.md changelog.
No code changed.

Recorded the user's refinement of the redundancy-vs-modularity question:
